memory of the shooting was \"stuck\" in his head and that he was on edge all the time, prompting him to accept a referral to a community mental health clinic offered through his church’s outreach program.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since I was there during that shooting, the scene keeps replaying in my head all day and I can’t shut it off.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S027 every little noise outside like someone’s about to start shooting again.\n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): It’s like a loop in my head—out of nowhere during a delivery or even sitting in church I suddenly see those people running past my van and hear the pops of the gun going off, and I just freeze and can’t think about anything else until it slowly fades.\n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I’ve been making It's like a loop in my head where I suddenly see the people running past my van and hear the gunshots, and I just freeze up and lose focus completely, whether I'm making a delivery or sitting in church.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you been avoiding places, people, conversations, or activities that remind you of it, or trying hard not to think about it?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been doing everything I can to avoid reminders. I'm actively avoiding any reminders of that night because of the intense fear and tension it brings, rather than because I feel detached.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month since the shooting, or have they continued for more than a month?"

They have definitely been present for less than a month. The shooting only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.

I'm also incredibly jumpy now—any sudden sound like a car backfires makes my whole body flinch and my heart race like crazy.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than a month since the shooting, or are these symptoms still only in the first few weeks after it?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been a month at all. The shooting just keeps replaying in my head and I jump at every little sound. I'm actively avoiding any reminders of that night because of the intense fear and tension it brings, rather than because I feel detached.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month since the shooting, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been present for less than a month. Also, have you had nightmares, trouble sleeping, or been extra jumpy since then?\n\nPatient: They've been staying constantly bad all week, and my sleep is completely ruined because as soon as I close my eyes I'm back in that parking lot, tossing and turning until I jerk awake at any little noise outside. I'm also incredibly jumpy now—any sudden sound like a car backfires makes my whole body flinch and my heart race like crazy.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than a month since the shooting, or are these symptoms still only in the first few weeks after it?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been a month at all. The shooting happened just about a week ago, so these symptoms have only been going on for this past week.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling emotionally numb, detached, or like the world around you was unreal since the shooting?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt numb or detached, and everything around me feels way too real. It's actually the opposite—I'm constantly anxious, highly on edge, and easily startled by any little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to feel numb, detached, or emotionally shut down since the shooting, or is it mainly the replaying, fear, and being on edge?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the latter, as I don't feel numb or shut down at all; instead, I feel completely overwhelmed and constantly on edge. The shooting just keeps replaying in my head and I jump at every little sound. I'm actively avoiding any reminders of that night because of the intense fear and tension it brings, rather than because I feel detached.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month since the shooting, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been present for less than a month. The shooting only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: Has it been less than one month since the shooting, or have these symptoms continued for more than one month?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been less than a month since the shooting. It only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel dazed, unreal, or like you were outside your body, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don't recall feeling dazed, unreal, or outside of my body during the shooting. I was just extremely terrified, though I do have some blank spaces in my memory from that night.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel like time slowed down, you were in a daze, or the event was unreal even for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall feeling like things slowed down or were unreal. It was just extremely chaotic and everything happened so fast while I was trying to hide. I don't think I was in a daze, just incredibly terrified."

The shooting just keeps replaying in my head and I jump at every little sound. I'm actively avoiding any reminders of that night because of the intense fear and tension it brings, rather than because I feel detached.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month since the shooting, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been present for less than a month. The shooting only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: Has it been less than one month since the shooting, or have these symptoms continued for more than one month?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been less than a month since the shooting. It only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel dazed, unreal, or like you were outside your body, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don't recall feeling dazed, unreal, or outside of my body during the shooting. I was just extremely terrified, though I do have some blank spaces in my memory from that night.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel like time slowed down, you were in a daze, or the event was unreal even for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall feeling like things slowed down or were unreal. It was just extremely chaotic and everything happened so fast while I was trying to hide. I don't think I was in a daze, just incredibly terrified.\n\nDoctor: During the shooting or right afterward, did you feel dazed, unreal, like time slowed down, or like you were outside your body even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall feeling like I was outside my body or that time slowed down in that way. I was mostly just terrified and focused on hiding behind my van. I do have some blank spots in my memory about the details of what happened, though."

It's actually the opposite—I'm constantly anxious, highly on edge, and easily startled by any little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to feel numb, detached, or emotionally shut down since the shooting, or is it mainly the replaying, fear, and being on edge?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the latter, as I don't feel numb or shut down at all; instead, I feel completely overwhelmed and constantly on edge. The shooting just keeps replaying in my head and I jump at every little sound. I'm actively avoiding any reminders of that night because of the intense fear and tension it brings, rather than because I feel detached.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month since the shooting, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been present for less than a month. The shooting only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: Has it been less than one month since the shooting, or have these symptoms continued for more than one month?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been less than a month since the shooting. It only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel dazed, unreal, or like you were outside your body, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don't recall feeling dazed, unreal, or outside of my body during the shooting. I was just extremely terrified, though I do have some blank spaces in my memory from that night.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel like time slowed down, you were in a daze, or the event was unreal even for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall feeling like things slowed down or were unreal. It was just extremely chaotic and everything happened so fast while I was trying to hide. I don't think I was in a daze, just incredibly terrified.\n\nDoctor: During the shooting or right afterward, did you feel dazed, unreal, like time slowed down, or like you were outside your body even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall feeling like I was outside my body or that time slowed down in that way. I was mostly just terrified and focused on hiding behind my van. I do have some blank spots in my memory about the details of what happened, though.\n\nDoctor: During the shooting or right afterward, did you feel dazed, unreal, like time slowed down, or like you were outside your body even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't feel like I was outside my body or that time slowed down. Everything was just a huge blur because of how fast it all happened, and I still have some blank spots in my memory from that night."

It's actually the opposite—I feel way too plugged in, constantly on edge and jumpy, and that scene just keeps replaying in my head.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you had any moments of feeling confused, in a daze, unable to remember parts of what happened, or like you were watching it from outside your body?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have some blank spaces in my memory from that night. I remember ducking behind my van and hearing people scream, but there are chunks missing, like how many shots were fired or exactly where the shooter was. No matter how hard I try to replay it, I just can't fill in those gaps.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you had any moments where you felt like you were outside your body, in a daze, or like the event was happening again in front of you?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel like I'm outside my body, but it definitely feels like the shooting is happening all over again. It's like a loop in my head where I suddenly see the people running past my van and hear the gunshots, and I just freeze up and lose focus completely, whether I'm making a delivery or sitting in church.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you been avoiding places, people, conversations, or activities that remind you of it, or trying hard not to think about it?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been doing everything I can to avoid reminders. I make excuses to skip my delivery routes on the Westside, won't go near apartment lots, and even stopped going to my basketball league and choir. If my mom or coworkers try to ask about that night, I shut them down immediately because just talking about it makes my chest tighten up.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been getting better, staying the same, or getting worse since the shooting? Also, have you had nightmares, trouble sleeping, or been extra jumpy since then?\n\nPatient: They've been staying constantly bad all week, and my sleep is completely ruined because as soon as I close my eyes I'm back in that parking lot, tossing and turning until I jerk awake at any little noise outside. I'm also incredibly jumpy now—any sudden sound like a car backfires makes my whole body flinch and my heart race like crazy.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than a month since the shooting, or are these symptoms still only in the first few weeks after it?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been a month at all. The shooting happened just about a week ago, so these symptoms have only been going on for this past week.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling emotionally numb, detached, or like the world around you was unreal since the shooting?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt numb or detached, and everything around me feels way too real. It's actually the opposite—I'm constantly anxious, highly on edge, and easily startled by any little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to feel numb, detached, or emotionally shut down since the shooting, or is it mainly the replaying, fear, and being on edge?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the latter, as I don't feel numb or shut down at all; instead, I feel completely overwhelmed and constantly on edge. The shooting just keeps replaying in my head and I jump at every little sound. I'm actively avoiding any reminders of that night because of the intense fear and tension it brings, rather than because I feel detached.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month since the shooting, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been present for less than a month. The shooting only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: Has it been less than one month since the shooting, or have these symptoms continued for more than one month?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been less than a month since the shooting. It only happened about a week ago, so I've been dealing with all of this for just this past week.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel dazed, unreal, or like you were outside your body, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don't recall feeling dazed, unreal, or outside of my body during the shooting. I was just extremely terrified, though I do have some blank spaces in my memory from that night.\n\nDoctor: During or right after the shooting, did you feel like time slowed down, you were in a daze, or the event was unreal even for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall feeling like things slowed down or were unreal. I was mostly just terrified and focused on hiding behind my van. Everything was just a huge blur because of how fast it all happened, and I still have some blank spots in my memory from that night.\n\nDoctor: Just to confirm, did your symptoms start within 4 weeks of the shooting and have you had any dissociative symptoms like feeling detached, unreal, or outside your body at any point since then?\n\nPatient: Yes, all of this started just a couple of days after the shooting, so it has only been about a week now. Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nThey have definitely been present for less than a month.
